But I'm not sitting around fretting about a whole bunch of different things, no.\n\nDoctor: Did something particularly stressful happen in your life around the time this brain fog started?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, yeah. About two weeks ago, my husband retired. So suddenly he's just... home all day. Right around then is when I noticed this sharper change—first my energy and confidence were kind of all over the place, and then it settled into this low, cloudy feeling I have now. It's been going on for those two weeks.\n\nDoctor: During that time when your energy and confidence were all over the place, did you feel unusually up or energized?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I did. There were a few days where I felt... well, almost too bright and bold, if that makes sense. Like at the association meetings, I'd catch myself just talking right over people. And then I'd go home and just snap at my husband over some small little thing. So it was definitely a different kind of energy for me.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I was still sleeping my regular hours. I'd go to bed around ten and wake up at six, just like always. I didn't feel like I needed less sleep, I just felt... different while I was awake.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, were you more active than usual or taking on more projects than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was. Just frustrated that I can't seem to get my work done like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm not much of a worrier by nature. The only thing I guess I worry about right now is just my job, since I'm falling behind with this brain fog. But I'm not sitting around fretting about a whole bunch of different things, no.\n\nDoctor: Did something particularly stressful happen in your life around the time this brain fog started?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, yeah. About two weeks ago, my husband retired. So suddenly he's just... home all day. Right around then is when I noticed this sharper change—first my energy and confidence were kind of all over the place, and then it settled into this low, cloudy feeling I have now. It's been going on for those two weeks.\n\nDoctor: During that time when your energy and confidence were all over the place, did you feel unusually up or energized?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I did. There were a few days where I felt... well, almost too bright and bold, if that makes sense. Like at the association meetings, I'd catch myself just talking right over people. And then I'd go home and just snap at my husband over some small little thing. So it was definitely a different kind of energy for me.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I was still sleeping my regular hours. I'd go to bed around ten and wake up at six, just like always. I didn't feel like I needed less sleep, I just felt... different while I was awake.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, were you more active than usual or taking on more projects than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was. I actually... I took on these extra green-energy proposals for several of the communities I manage, all at the exact same time. I just kept setting up calls and scheduling meetings for it, even though my calendar was already completely full. I just kept adding more to my plate.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, did you find yourself talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I was talking more than usual. I mean, I mentioned talking over people at the meetings, but that was more about... well, just being impatient, I guess. Not really that I was talking a lot more overall. Just a bit more forceful when I did.\n\nDoctor: During those few days when you felt unusually bright and bold, did your thoughts feel like they were racing?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely did. My mind was just... it was going a mile a minute. I'd be thinking about the solar panels for the new proposal, and then my brain would just jump straight to the community budgets, and then suddenly I'm thinking about my husband being home. It was like my thoughts were moving so fast I couldn't even catch hold of one idea long enough to actually finish it.\n\nDoctor: During those few days, did you feel unusually confident or important compared with your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I started feeling like I could fix every building problem in Denver if people would just let me run with my ideas. I mean, that's really not how I usually talk about myself or my work, but I just felt so sure I had all the answers.\n\nDoctor: During those few days, were you more driven than usual to get things done?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely was. I actually took on extra green-energy proposals for several of the communities I manage, all at the exact same time. I just kept setting up calls and... well, booking more meetings, even when my calendar was already completely full. I just felt like I had to get all of it moving right then.\n\nDoctor: During those unusually bright and bold days, did your behavior create serious problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not serious problems. I mean, I'm still getting to work every day. But I have had to slow down on making decisions. I've actually asked my assistant to double-check all the vendor details for me, and... well, I put off a couple of items for the board. I just don't really trust my own judgment right now, so I'm trying to be careful.\n\nDoctor: During those unusually bright and bold days, were you more active than usual or taking on more projects than normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was. I just had this... this push in my body, like I had to keep moving. I was constantly calling people, making lists, and just walking the properties even after I should have gone home for the day.\n\nDoctor: During those unusually bright and bold days, did you feel more driven than usual to get things done?\n\nPatient: I think we just went over that a minute ago, actually. But yes, like I was saying, I was definitely a lot more driven. I just felt like I had to get all those extra proposals and meetings done right then and there. It was a very strong push to just finish everything.\n\nDoctor: During those unusually bright and bold days, were you more easily distracted than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, with all those projects and lists I was working on, I was actually pretty focused on what I was doing. I didn't really notice my mind wandering off or having trouble paying attention to things.\n\nDoctor: During those unusually bright and bold days, did you do anything risky or out of character that could have caused problems for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I did do something at work that I probably shouldn't have. I told a couple of our vendors to go ahead and hold some equipment for us, and even start the paperwork on it, before I had actually checked the budgets to see if we could afford it. Now I'm a bit worried that I might have put us in a bad spot financially.\n\nDoctor: Before this recent spell, have you ever had another period where you felt unusually energized, bold, or driven like that?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think so. I've never had a period like this before.\n\nDoctor: How many days did that unusually bright and driven period last?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I didn't... I wasn't really keeping track of the days while it was happening. It's hard to say exactly how long it lasted, so I couldn't really give you an exact number.\n\nDoctor: Did that unusually bright and driven period last at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned, I really wasn't watching the calendar. It might have been four days straight, but I'd just be guessing. I was so caught up in getting things done with the properties and the vendors that the days kind of just blurred together. I really couldn't tell you for sure if it was that long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
